### Code

```python
  1 | """
  2 | Train the C++ hybrid tokenizer: fixed C++ vocab + learned BPE, BERT-style whitespace.
  3 | 
  4 | Usage:
  5 |     python -m scripts.tok_train_cpp [--input data/cpp_clean.jsonl] [--vocab_size 65536] [--max_chars 5000000000]
  6 | 
  7 | See docs/design/01-tokenizer.md for full design.
  8 | 
  9 | Fixed vocabulary structure (4800 tokens, IDs 0-4799):
 10 |     - IDs 0-19: Special tokens (20)
 11 |     - IDs 20-199: C++ keywords (180)
 12 |     - IDs 200-399: Operators (200)
 13 |     - IDs 400-499: Preprocessor (100)
 14 |     - IDs 500-699: Punctuation (200)
 15 |     - IDs 700-3699: STL/Standard library functions (3000)
 16 |     - IDs 3700-4699: Numbers 0-999 (1000)
 17 |     - IDs 4700-4799: Reserved (100)
 18 |     - IDs 4800-65535: Learned BPE (60736)
 19 | """
 20 | import os
 21 | import json
 22 | import time
 23 | import argparse
 24 | from typing import Protocol, cast
 25 | 
 26 | from tokenizers import Tokenizer, Regex
 27 | from tokenizers.models import BPE
 28 | from tokenizers.trainers import BpeTrainer
 29 | from tokenizers.pre_tokenizers import Sequence, Split, WhitespaceSplit
 30 | 
 31 | 
 32 | # =============================================================================
 33 | # Load Fixed Vocabulary from JSON
 34 | # =============================================================================
 35 | 
 36 | def load_fixed_vocab(vocab_path: str) -> list[str]:
 37 |     """Load fixed vocabulary from JSON file. Returns ordered list where index = token ID."""
 38 |     with open(vocab_path, 'r') as f:
 39 |         vocab_dict = json.load(f)
 40 |     # Sort by ID to get ordered list
 41 |     max_id = max(vocab_dict.values())
 42 |     fixed_tokens = [''] * (max_id + 1)
 43 |     for token, id_ in vocab_dict.items():
 44 |         fixed_tokens[id_] = token
 45 |     return fixed_tokens
 46 | 
 47 | 
 48 | def build_fixed_tokens(vocab_path: str | None = None) -> list[str]:
 49 |     """Build ordered list of all fixed tokens. Index = token ID."""
 50 |     if vocab_path is None:
 51 |         # Default path relative to script location
 52 |         script_dir = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
 53 |         vocab_path = os.path.join(script_dir, '..', 'data', 'cpp_tokenizer', 'fixed_vocab.json')
 54 |     return load_fixed_vocab(vocab_path)
 55 | 
 56 | 
 57 | # =============================================================================
 58 | # Pre-tokenizer: BERT-style whitespace + C++ operator isolation
 59 | # =============================================================================
 60 | 
 61 | CPP_PRE_TOKENIZER_PATTERN = r"""(?x)
 62 |     # Multi-char operators (longest match first)
 63 |     <=>|<<=|>>=|->\*|\.\*|
 64 |     ::|->|==|!=|<=|>=|&&|\|\||<<|>>|\+\+|--|
 65 |     \+=|-=|\*=|/=|%=|&=|\|=|\^=|
 66 |     \.\.\.|\#\#|//|/\*|\*/|
 67 |     # Diff markers
 68 |     \@\@|---|\+\+\+|
 69 |     # Newlines (structural whitespace token)
 70 |     \n\n|\n|
 71 |     # Single-char punctuation (each is its own token)
 72 |     [{}()\[\]<>;:,.\+\-\*/%&\|^~!?=\#@\$_\\\"'`]
 73 | """
 74 | 
 75 | def build_pre_tokenizer():
 76 |     """BERT-style: isolate fixed patterns then split on whitespace."""
 77 |     return Sequence([
 78 |         Split(Regex(CPP_PRE_TOKENIZER_PATTERN), behavior="isolated"),
 79 |         WhitespaceSplit(),
 80 |     ])
 81 | 
 82 | 
 83 | class _TokenizerWithPreTokenizer(Protocol):
 84 |     pre_tokenizer: object
 85 | 
 86 | 
 87 | class _BpeTrainerFactory(Protocol):
 88 |     def __call__(
 89 |         self,
 90 |         *,
 91 |         vocab_size: int,
 92 |         min_frequency: int,
 93 |         special_tokens: list[str],
 94 |         show_progress: bool,
 95 |     ) -> BpeTrainer: ...
 96 | 
 97 | 
 98 | # =============================================================================
 99 | # Text iterator from JSONL
100 | # =============================================================================
101 | 
102 | def text_iterator(input_path: str, max_chars: int, doc_cap: int = 10_000):
103 |     nchars = 0
104 |     with open(input_path, 'r') as f:
105 |         for line in f:
106 |             if not line.strip():
107 |                 continue
108 |             record = json.loads(line)
109 |             text = record.get('text', '')
110 |             if len(text) > doc_cap:
111 |                 text = text[:doc_cap]
112 |             nchars += len(text)
113 |             yield text
114 |             if nchars >= max_chars:
115 |                 return
116 |     print(f"Read {nchars:,} chars from {input_path}")
117 | 
118 | 
119 | # =============================================================================
120 | # Main: train tokenizer
121 | # =============================================================================
122 | 
123 | def main():
124 |     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Train C++ hybrid BPE tokenizer')
125 |     parser.add_argument('--input', default='data/cpp_clean.jsonl', help='Input JSONL file')
126 |     parser.add_argument('--output_dir', default='data/cpp_tokenizer', help='Output directory')
127 |     parser.add_argument('--vocab_size', type=int, default=65536, help='Total vocab size (default: 65536)')
128 |     parser.add_argument('--max_chars', type=int, default=5_000_000_000, help='Max chars to train on')
129 |     parser.add_argument('--doc_cap', type=int, default=10_000, help='Max chars per document')
130 |     parser.add_argument('--min_frequency', type=int, default=2, help='Min BPE merge frequency')
131 |     parser.add_argument('--fixed_vocab', default=None, help='Path to fixed_vocab.json (default: data/cpp_tokenizer/fixed_vocab.json)')
132 |     args = parser.parse_args()
133 | 
134 |     # Resolve paths relative to project root
135 |     project_root = os.path.abspath(os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), '..'))
136 |     input_path = os.path.join(project_root, args.input)
137 |     output_dir = os.path.join(project_root, args.output_dir)
138 |     os.makedirs(output_dir, exist_ok=True)
139 | 
140 |     # Resolve fixed vocab path
141 |     if args.fixed_vocab:
142 |         fixed_vocab_path = args.fixed_vocab
143 |     else:
144 |         fixed_vocab_path = os.path.join(output_dir, 'fixed_vocab.json')
145 | 
146 |     print(f"Input: {input_path}")
147 |     print(f"Output: {output_dir}")
148 |     print(f"Fixed vocab: {fixed_vocab_path}")
149 |     print(f"Vocab size: {args.vocab_size}")
150 |     print(f"Max chars: {args.max_chars:,}")
151 | 
152 |     # Build fixed tokens from JSON
153 |     fixed_tokens = build_fixed_tokens(fixed_vocab_path)
154 |     print(f"Fixed tokens: {len(fixed_tokens)}")
155 |     print(f"Learned BPE slots: {args.vocab_size - len(fixed_tokens)}")
156 | 
157 |     # Initialize tokenizer
158 |     tokenizer = Tokenizer(BPE(unk_token="<UNK>"))
159 |     cast(_TokenizerWithPreTokenizer, tokenizer).pre_tokenizer = build_pre_tokenizer()
160 | 
161 |     # Train BPE
162 |     trainer = cast(_BpeTrainerFactory, BpeTrainer)(
163 |         vocab_size=args.vocab_size,
164 |         min_frequency=args.min_frequency,
165 |         special_tokens=fixed_tokens,  # These get IDs 0..1599 in order
166 |         show_progress=True,
167 |     )
168 | 
169 |     print("\n=== Training BPE ===")
170 |     t0 = time.time()
171 |     text_iter = text_iterator(input_path, args.max_chars, args.doc_cap)
172 |     tokenizer.train_from_iterator(text_iter, trainer=trainer)
173 |     t1 = time.time()
174 |     print(f"Training time: {t1 - t0:.1f}s")
175 |     print(f"Vocab size: {tokenizer.get_vocab_size()}")
176 | 
177 |     # Save
178 |     tokenizer_path = os.path.join(output_dir, "tokenizer.json")
179 |     tokenizer.save(tokenizer_path)
180 |     print(f"Saved to {tokenizer_path}")
181 | 
182 |     # === Validation ===
183 |     print("\n=== Validation ===")
184 |     validate(tokenizer, fixed_tokens)
185 | 
186 | 
187 | def validate(tokenizer, fixed_tokens):
188 |     """Run validation checks for the tokenizer."""
189 |     fixed_map = {tok: i for i, tok in enumerate(fixed_tokens) if tok}
190 | 
191 |     tests = [
192 |         # (input, description, expected_properties)
193 |         ("std::vector<int>", "STL template", {
194 |             "max_tokens": 7,  # std :: vector < int > (6 meaningful)
195 |         }),
196 |         ("nullptr", "keyword as single token", {
197 |             "exact_tokens": 1,
198 |         }),
199 |         ("return 42;", "keyword + number + punct", {
200 |             "max_tokens": 5,
201 |         }),
202 |         ("cout << endl", "stream output", {
203 |             "max_tokens": 5,
204 |         }),
205 |         ("if (!buf) return;", "null check pattern", {
206 |             "max_tokens": 10,
207 |         }),
208 |         ("cudaMalloc(&ptr, size)", "CUDA API call", {
209 |             "max_tokens": 10,
210 |         }),
211 |         ("std::unique_ptr<T>", "smart pointer", {
212 |             "max_tokens": 7,
213 |         }),
214 |     ]
215 | 
216 |     all_passed = True
217 |     for text, desc, props in tests:
218 |         enc = tokenizer.encode(text)
219 |         ids = enc.ids
220 |         tokens = enc.tokens
221 |         print(f"\n  {desc}: \"{text}\"")
222 |         print(f"    Tokens ({len(ids)}): {tokens}")
223 |         print(f"    IDs: {ids}")
224 | 
225 |         if "exact_tokens" in props and len(ids) != props["exact_tokens"]:
226 |             print(f"    FAIL: expected {props['exact_tokens']} tokens, got {len(ids)}")
227 |             all_passed = False
228 |         if "max_tokens" in props and len(ids) > props["max_tokens"]:
229 |             print(f"    FAIL: expected <= {props['max_tokens']} tokens, got {len(ids)}")
230 |             all_passed = False
231 | 
232 |     # Check fixed token IDs
233 |     print("\n  Fixed token ID checks:")
234 |     checks = [
235 |         # (token, category, expected_id_range_start, expected_id_range_end)
236 |         ("::", "operator", 200, 400),
237 |         ("->", "operator", 200, 400),
238 |         ("nullptr", "keyword", 20, 200),
239 |         ("return", "keyword", 20, 200),
240 |         ("std", "STL name", 700, 3700),
241 |         ("vector", "STL name", 700, 3700),
242 |         ("cudaMalloc", "CUDA API", 700, 3700),
243 |         ("42", "number", 3700, 4700),
244 |         ("0", "number", 3700, 4700),
245 |         ("999", "number", 3700, 4700),
246 |     ]
247 |     vocab = tokenizer.get_vocab()
248 |     for tok, cat, range_start, range_end in checks:
249 |         if tok in fixed_map:
250 |             expected_id = fixed_map[tok]
251 |             actual_id = vocab.get(tok, -1)
252 |             in_range = range_start <= actual_id < range_end
253 |             status = "OK" if actual_id == expected_id and in_range else f"MISMATCH (expected {expected_id}, got {actual_id})"
254 |             print(f"    {tok!r:20s} ({cat:10s}) ID={actual_id:5d} {status}")
255 |             if actual_id != expected_id:
256 |                 all_passed = False
257 |         else:
258 |             print(f"    {tok!r:20s} ({cat:10s}) NOT in fixed vocab!")
259 |             # Not a failure if not in fixed vocab - might be learned
260 | 
261 |     # Decode test
262 |     print("\n  Decode test:")
263 |     test_code = "int main() {\nreturn 0;\n}"
264 |     enc = tokenizer.encode(test_code)
265 |     decoded = tokenizer.decode(enc.ids)
266 |     print(f"    Input:   {test_code!r}")
267 |     print(f"    Decoded: {decoded!r}")
268 |     print(f"    Match: {decoded.strip() == test_code.strip()}")
269 | 
270 |     # Efficiency test
271 |     print("\n  Efficiency comparison:")
272 |     sample = """#include <iostream>
273 | #include <vector>
274 | #include <string>
275 | 
276 | int main() {
277 | std::vector<std::string> names;
278 | names.push_back("hello");
279 | names.push_back("world");
280 | 
281 | for (const auto& name : names) {
282 | std::cout << name << std::endl;
283 | }
284 | 
285 | return 0;
286 | }"""
287 |     enc = tokenizer.encode(sample)
288 |     bytes_count = len(sample.encode('utf-8'))
289 |     tokens_count = len(enc.ids)
290 |     print(f"    Sample: {bytes_count} bytes -> {tokens_count} tokens")
291 |     print(f"    Tokens/byte: {tokens_count/bytes_count:.3f}")
292 |     print(f"    Bytes/token: {bytes_count/tokens_count:.1f}")
293 | 
294 |     # Fixed vocab stats
295 |     print("\n  Fixed vocab coverage:")
296 |     print(f"    Total fixed tokens: {len(fixed_tokens)}")
297 |     print("    Keywords (20-199): 180 slots")
298 |     print("    Operators (200-399): 200 slots")
299 |     print("    Preprocessor (400-499): 100 slots")
300 |     print("    Punctuation (500-699): 200 slots")
301 |     print("    STL/stdlib (700-3699): 3000 slots")
302 |     print("    Numbers (3700-4699): 1000 slots")
303 |     print("    Reserved (4700-4799): 100 slots")
304 | 
305 |     if all_passed:
306 |         print("\n  ALL CHECKS PASSED")
307 |     else:
308 |         print("\n  SOME CHECKS FAILED")
309 | 
310 | 
311 | if __name__ == '__main__':
312 |     main()
```

### Code

```python
   1 | #!/usr/bin/env python3
   2 | """Analyze proposed tokenizer v3 vocabulary frequency in C++ training corpus.
   3 | 
   4 | Validates proposed fixed vocabulary tokens from TOKENIZER_V2_PROPOSAL.md against
   5 | actual usage in the cpp_compilable training dataset. Reports per-category coverage
   6 | and per-token frequency to guide fixed vocab allocation.
   7 | 
   8 | Usage:
   9 |     # Analyze single shard (fast, ~2 min)
  10 |     python -m scripts.data.analyze_vocab_frequency /tmp/shard_00000.parquet
  11 | 
  12 |     # Analyze multiple shards
  13 |     python -m scripts.data.analyze_vocab_frequency /tmp/shard_0000*.parquet
  14 | 
  15 |     # Filter by category
  16 |     python -m scripts.data.analyze_vocab_frequency /tmp/shard_00000.parquet -c cuda
  17 | 
  18 |     # Show morpheme analysis
  19 |     python -m scripts.data.analyze_vocab_frequency /tmp/shard_00000.parquet --morphemes
  20 | 
  21 |     # JSON output for downstream processing
  22 |     python -m scripts.data.analyze_vocab_frequency /tmp/shard_00000.parquet --json -o results.json
  23 | """
  24 | 
  25 | import argparse
  26 | import glob
  27 | import json
  28 | import os
  29 | import re
  30 | import sys
  31 | import time

  33 | from concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor, as_completed
  34 | from typing import TypedDict
  35 | 
  36 | import pyarrow.parquet as pq

1051 |         "src",
1052 |         "dst",
1053 |     ],
1054 | }
1055 | 
1056 | # ─── Token Extraction ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
1057 | 
1058 | # C++ identifiers including namespace-qualified (std::vector, google::protobuf::Message)
1059 | IDENT_RE = re.compile(r"[a-zA-Z_]\w*(?:::\w+)*")
1060 | 
1061 | # $-prefixed operators (MongoDB)
1062 | DOLLAR_RE = re.compile(r"\$[a-zA-Z_]\w*")
1063 | 
1064 | # String literal contents (for SQL keyword detection)
1065 | # Handles escaped quotes inside strings
1066 | STRING_RE = re.compile(r'"((?:[^"\\]|\\.)*)"')
1067 | 
1068 | 
1069 | class _DocumentAnalysis(TypedDict):
1070 |     ident_counts: Counter[str]
1071 |     dollar_counts: Counter[str]
1072 |     sql_keyword_counts: Counter[str]
1073 |     morpheme_counts: Counter[str]
1074 | 
1075 | 
1076 | class _MergedAnalysis(_DocumentAnalysis):
1077 |     num_docs: int
1078 |     doc_presence: Counter[str]
1079 | 
1080 | 
1081 | # Camel/snake case splitter for morpheme analysis
1082 | def split_identifier(ident: str) -> list[str]:
1083 |     """Split a C++ identifier into morpheme components."""
1084 |     parts: list[str] = []
1085 |     for chunk in ident.split("_"):
1086 |         if not chunk:
1087 |             continue
1088 |         # Split camelCase: maxActiveBlocks -> [max, Active, Blocks]
1089 |         # Split UPPER runs: HTTPSConnection -> [HTTPS, Connection]
1090 |         tokens = re.findall(r"[A-Z]+(?=[A-Z][a-z])|[A-Z]?[a-z]+|[A-Z]+|[0-9]+", chunk)
1091 |         parts.extend(t.lower() for t in tokens if len(t) > 1)
1092 |     return parts
1093 | 
1094 | 
1095 | # ─── Document Processing ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
1096 | 
1097 | 
1098 | def analyze_document(text: str) -> _DocumentAnalysis:
1099 |     """Analyze a single C++ document for proposed vocabulary tokens.
1100 | 
1101 |     Returns dict with:
1102 |         ident_counts: Counter of all identifiers
1103 |         dollar_counts: Counter of $-prefixed operators
1104 |         sql_keyword_counts: Counter of SQL keywords found in string literals
1105 |         morpheme_counts: Counter of identifier morpheme components
1106 |     """
1107 |     # Pass 1: Extract all C++ identifiers
1108 |     ident_counts = Counter(IDENT_RE.findall(text))
1109 | 
1110 |     # Pass 2: Extract $-prefixed operators
1111 |     dollar_counts = Counter(DOLLAR_RE.findall(text))
1112 | 
1113 |     # Pass 3: Extract SQL keywords from string literals
1114 |     sql_keyword_counts: Counter[str] = Counter()
1115 |     for string_content in STRING_RE.findall(text):
1116 |         # SQL keywords are typically uppercase, 2+ chars
1117 |         for word in re.findall(r"\b[A-Z][A-Z_]{1,}\b", string_content):
1118 |             sql_keyword_counts[word] += 1
1119 | 
1120 |     # Pass 4: Morpheme analysis - split identifiers into components
1121 |     morpheme_counts: Counter[str] = Counter()
1122 |     for ident in ident_counts:
1123 |         if len(ident) > 3:  # Skip trivially short identifiers
1124 |             freq = ident_counts[ident]
1125 |             for part in split_identifier(ident):
1126 |                 morpheme_counts[part] += freq
1127 | 
1128 |     return {
1129 |         "ident_counts": ident_counts,
1130 |         "dollar_counts": dollar_counts,
1131 |         "sql_keyword_counts": sql_keyword_counts,
1132 |         "morpheme_counts": morpheme_counts,
1133 |     }
1134 | 
1135 | 
1136 | def process_row_group(args):
1137 |     """Process a single parquet row group. Designed for multiprocessing."""
1138 |     parquet_path, rg_idx = args
1139 |     pf = pq.ParquetFile(parquet_path)
1140 |     rg = pf.read_row_group(rg_idx)
1141 |     texts = rg.column("text").to_pylist()
1142 | 
1143 |     merged: _MergedAnalysis = {
1144 |         "ident_counts": Counter(),
1145 |         "dollar_counts": Counter(),
1146 |         "sql_keyword_counts": Counter(),
1147 |         "morpheme_counts": Counter(),
1148 |         "num_docs": len(texts),
1149 |         "doc_presence": Counter(),  # token -> num docs containing it
1150 |     }
1151 | 
1152 |     for text in texts:
1153 |         result = analyze_document(text)
1154 |         for key in (
1155 |             "ident_counts",
1156 |             "dollar_counts",
1157 |             "sql_keyword_counts",
1158 |             "morpheme_counts",
1159 |         ):
1160 |             merged[key] += result[key]
1161 | 
1162 |         # Track document presence (for doc_freq calculation)
1163 |         seen = set()
1164 |         for key in ("ident_counts", "dollar_counts", "sql_keyword_counts"):
1165 |             seen.update(result[key].keys())
1166 |         for token in seen:
1167 |             merged["doc_presence"][token] += 1
1168 | 
1169 |     return merged
1170 | 
1171 | 
1172 | # ─── Results Aggregation ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
1173 | 
1174 | 
1175 | def aggregate_results(results: list[_MergedAnalysis]) -> _MergedAnalysis:
1176 |     """Merge results from multiple row groups."""
1177 |     merged: _MergedAnalysis = {
1178 |         "ident_counts": Counter(),
1179 |         "dollar_counts": Counter(),
1180 |         "sql_keyword_counts": Counter(),
1181 |         "morpheme_counts": Counter(),
1182 |         "num_docs": 0,
1183 |         "doc_presence": Counter(),
1184 |     }
1185 |     for r in results:
1186 |         for key in (
1187 |             "ident_counts",
1188 |             "dollar_counts",
1189 |             "sql_keyword_counts",
1190 |             "morpheme_counts",
1191 |         ):
1192 |             merged[key] += r[key]
1193 |         merged["num_docs"] += r["num_docs"]
1194 |         merged["doc_presence"] += Counter(r["doc_presence"])
1195 |     return merged
1196 | 
1197 | 
1198 | def lookup_proposed_vocab(merged: _MergedAnalysis) -> dict[str, list[dict[str, int | str]]]:
1199 |     """Look up each proposed vocab token in the merged results."""
1200 |     results: dict[str, list[dict[str, int | str]]] = {}
1201 |     for category, (match_mode, tokens) in PROPOSED_VOCAB.items():
1202 |         category_results: list[dict[str, int | str]] = []
1203 |         for token in tokens:
1204 |             if match_mode == "ident":
1205 |                 count = merged["ident_counts"].get(token, 0)
1206 |                 doc_count = merged["doc_presence"].get(token, 0)
1207 |             elif match_mode == "dollar":
1208 |                 count = merged["dollar_counts"].get(token, 0)
1209 |                 doc_count = merged["doc_presence"].get(token, 0)
1210 |             elif match_mode == "sql_string":
1211 |                 count = merged["sql_keyword_counts"].get(token, 0)
1212 |                 doc_count = merged["doc_presence"].get(token, 0)
1213 |             else:
1214 |                 count = 0
1215 |                 doc_count = 0
1216 | 
1217 |             category_results.append(
1218 |                 {
1219 |                     "token": token,
1220 |                     "count": count,
1221 |                     "doc_count": doc_count,
1222 |                 }
1223 |             )
1224 | 
1225 |         # Sort by count descending
1226 |         category_results.sort(key=lambda x: x["count"], reverse=True)
1227 |         results[category] = category_results
1228 | 
1229 |     return results
1230 | 
1231 | 
1232 | def lookup_morphemes(merged: _MergedAnalysis) -> dict[str, list[dict[str, int | str]]]:
1233 |     """Look up morpheme stems in the morpheme analysis results."""
1234 |     results: dict[str, list[dict[str, int | str]]] = {}
1235 |     for category, stems in MORPHEME_STEMS.items():
1236 |         category_results: list[dict[str, int | str]] = []
1237 |         for stem in stems:
1238 |             count = merged["morpheme_counts"].get(stem, 0)
1239 |             category_results.append({"stem": stem, "count": count})
1240 |         category_results.sort(key=lambda x: x["count"], reverse=True)
1241 |         results[category] = category_results
1242 |     return results
1243 | 
1244 | 
1245 | # ─── Output Formatting ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
1246 | 
1247 | 
1248 | def print_category_report(category: str, results: list[dict], num_docs: int):
1249 |     """Print a formatted report for one category."""
1250 |     found = sum(1 for r in results if r["count"] > 0)
1251 |     total = len(results)
1252 |     total_hits = sum(r["count"] for r in results)
1253 |     coverage = found / total * 100 if total > 0 else 0
1254 | 
1255 |     print(f"\n{'═' * 78}")
1256 |     print(
1257 |         f"  {category.upper().replace('_', ' ')}  ({found}/{total} found, {coverage:.0f}% coverage)"
1258 |     )
1259 |     print(f"  Total occurrences: {total_hits:,}")
1260 |     print(f"{'═' * 78}")
1261 | 
1262 |     if not results:
1263 |         print("  (no tokens defined)")
1264 |         return
1265 | 
1266 |     # Header
1267 |     "token" if "token" in results[0] else "stem"
1268 |     print(f"  {'Token':<45s} {'Count':>10s} {'Docs':>8s} {'Doc%':>7s}  Status")
1269 |     print(f"  {'─' * 45} {'─' * 10} {'─' * 8} {'─' * 7}  {'─' * 12}")
1270 | 
1271 |     for r in results:
1272 |         token = r.get("token", r.get("stem", "?"))
1273 |         count = r["count"]
1274 |         doc_count = r.get("doc_count", 0)
1275 |         doc_pct = doc_count / num_docs * 100 if num_docs > 0 else 0
1276 | 
1277 |         if count == 0:
1278 |             status = "NOT FOUND"
1279 |         elif count < 10:
1280 |             status = "RARE"
1281 |         elif count < 100:
1282 |             status = "LOW"
1283 |         elif count < 1000:
1284 |             status = "MODERATE"
1285 |         elif count < 10000:
1286 |             status = "HIGH"
1287 |         else:
1288 |             status = "VERY HIGH"
1289 | 
1290 |         print(
1291 |             f"  {token:<45s} {count:>10,d} {doc_count:>8,d} {doc_pct:>6.1f}%  {status}"
1292 |         )
1293 | 
1294 | 
1295 | def print_summary(vocab_results: dict, morpheme_results: dict, num_docs: int):
1296 |     """Print overall summary across all categories."""
1297 |     print(f"\n{'━' * 78}")
1298 |     print(f"  OVERALL SUMMARY  ({num_docs:,} documents analyzed)")
1299 |     print(f"{'━' * 78}")
1300 | 
1301 |     print(
1302 |         f"\n  {'Category':<30s} {'Proposed':>8s} {'Found':>8s} {'Coverage':>8s} {'Total Hits':>12s}"
1303 |     )
1304 |     print(f"  {'─' * 30} {'─' * 8} {'─' * 8} {'─' * 8} {'─' * 12}")
1305 | 
1306 |     grand_proposed = 0
1307 |     grand_found = 0
1308 |     grand_hits = 0
1309 | 
1310 |     for category, results in vocab_results.items():
1311 |         total = len(results)
1312 |         found = sum(1 for r in results if r["count"] > 0)
1313 |         hits = sum(r["count"] for r in results)
1314 |         coverage = found / total * 100 if total > 0 else 0
1315 | 
1316 |         grand_proposed += total
1317 |         grand_found += found
1318 |         grand_hits += hits
1319 | 
1320 |         print(
1321 |             f"  {category:<30s} {total:>8d} {found:>8d} {coverage:>7.0f}% {hits:>12,d}"
1322 |         )
1323 | 
1324 |     print(f"  {'─' * 30} {'─' * 8} {'─' * 8} {'─' * 8} {'─' * 12}")
1325 |     grand_coverage = grand_found / grand_proposed * 100 if grand_proposed > 0 else 0
1326 |     print(
1327 |         f"  {'TOTAL':<30s} {grand_proposed:>8d} {grand_found:>8d} {grand_coverage:>7.0f}% {grand_hits:>12,d}"
1328 |     )
1329 | 
1330 |     if morpheme_results:
1331 |         print("\n  MORPHEME ANALYSIS:")
1332 |         for category, results in morpheme_results.items():
1333 |             found = sum(1 for r in results if r["count"] > 0)
1334 |             total = len(results)
1335 |             hits = sum(r["count"] for r in results)
1336 |             print(
1337 |                 f"  {category:<30s} {total:>8d} {found:>8d} {found / total * 100:>7.0f}% {hits:>12,d}"
1338 |             )

1341 | # ─── Main ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
1342 | 
1343 | 
1344 | def main():
1345 |     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
1346 |         description="Analyze proposed tokenizer v3 vocabulary frequency in C++ corpus"
1347 |     )
1348 |     parser.add_argument(
1349 |         "parquet_files", nargs="+", help="Parquet file(s) or glob pattern"
1350 |     )
1351 |     parser.add_argument(
1352 |         "-c",
1353 |         "--category",
1354 |         type=str,
1355 |         default=None,
1356 |         help="Only show specific category (partial match)",
1357 |     )
1358 |     parser.add_argument(
1359 |         "--morphemes", action="store_true", help="Include morpheme stem analysis"
1360 |     )
1361 |     parser.add_argument(
1362 |         "--top", type=int, default=None, help="Only show top N tokens per category"
1363 |     )
1364 |     parser.add_argument(
1365 |         "-w", "--workers", type=int, default=4, help="Number of parallel workers"
1366 |     )
1367 |     parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true", help="Output results as JSON")
1368 |     parser.add_argument(
1369 |         "-o",
1370 |         "--output",
1371 |         type=str,
1372 |         default=None,
1373 |         help="Write results to file instead of stdout",
1374 |     )
1375 |     parser.add_argument(
1376 |         "--max-row-groups",
1377 |         type=int,
1378 |         default=None,
1379 |         help="Limit row groups processed per file (for quick testing)",
1380 |     )
1381 |     args = parser.parse_args()
1382 | 
1383 |     # Expand glob patterns
1384 |     parquet_files = []
1385 |     for pattern in args.parquet_files:
1386 |         expanded = glob.glob(pattern)
1387 |         if expanded:
1388 |             parquet_files.extend(expanded)
1389 |         elif os.path.isdir(pattern):
1390 |             parquet_files.extend(glob.glob(os.path.join(pattern, "*.parquet")))
1391 |         else:
1392 |             print(f"Warning: {pattern} not found", file=sys.stderr)
1393 |     parquet_files = sorted(set(parquet_files))
1394 | 
1395 |     if not parquet_files:
1396 |         print("Error: No parquet files found", file=sys.stderr)
1397 |         sys.exit(1)
1398 | 
1399 |     print(f"Analyzing {len(parquet_files)} parquet file(s)...")
1400 |     for f in parquet_files:
1401 |         sz = os.path.getsize(f) / (1024 * 1024)
1402 |         num_rg = pq.ParquetFile(f).metadata.num_row_groups
1403 |         print(f"  {os.path.basename(f)}: {sz:.0f} MB, {num_rg} row groups")
1404 | 
1405 |     # Build work items
1406 |     work_items = []
1407 |     for pf_path in parquet_files:
1408 |         pf = pq.ParquetFile(pf_path)
1409 |         num_rg = pf.metadata.num_row_groups
1410 |         if args.max_row_groups:
1411 |             num_rg = min(num_rg, args.max_row_groups)
1412 |         for rg_idx in range(num_rg):
1413 |             work_items.append((pf_path, rg_idx))
1414 | 
1415 |     print(f"Processing {len(work_items)} row groups with {args.workers} workers...")
1416 |     t0 = time.time()
1417 | 
1418 |     # Process in parallel
1419 |     all_results = []
1420 |     done = 0
1421 |     with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=args.workers) as executor:
1422 |         futures = {
1423 |             executor.submit(process_row_group, item): item for item in work_items
1424 |         }
1425 |         for future in as_completed(futures):
1426 |             result = future.result()
1427 |             all_results.append(result)
1428 |             done += 1
1429 |             if done % 10 == 0 or done == len(work_items):
1430 |                 elapsed = time.time() - t0
1431 |                 docs = sum(r["num_docs"] for r in all_results)
1432 |                 print(
1433 |                     f"  [{done}/{len(work_items)}] {docs:,} docs, {elapsed:.1f}s",
1434 |                     file=sys.stderr,
1435 |                 )
1436 | 
1437 |     elapsed = time.time() - t0
1438 | 
1439 |     # Aggregate
1440 |     merged = aggregate_results(all_results)
1441 |     num_docs = merged["num_docs"]
1442 |     print(
1443 |         f"\nCompleted: {num_docs:,} documents in {elapsed:.1f}s ({num_docs / elapsed:.0f} docs/s)"
1444 |     )
1445 | 
1446 |     # Lookup proposed vocab
1447 |     vocab_results = lookup_proposed_vocab(merged)
1448 |     morpheme_results = lookup_morphemes(merged) if args.morphemes else {}
1449 | 
1450 |     # Filter by category if requested
1451 |     if args.category:
1452 |         cat_filter = args.category.lower()
1453 |         vocab_results = {
1454 |             k: v for k, v in vocab_results.items() if cat_filter in k.lower()
1455 |         }
1456 |         morpheme_results = {
1457 |             k: v for k, v in morpheme_results.items() if cat_filter in k.lower()
1458 |         }
1459 | 
1460 |     # Truncate to top N if requested
1461 |     if args.top:
1462 |         vocab_results = {k: v[: args.top] for k, v in vocab_results.items()}
1463 |         morpheme_results = {k: v[: args.top] for k, v in morpheme_results.items()}
1464 | 
1465 |     if args.json:
1466 |         output = {
1467 |             "num_docs": num_docs,
1468 |             "elapsed_s": elapsed,
1469 |             "vocab": vocab_results,
1470 |             "morphemes": morpheme_results,
1471 |         }
1472 |         text = json.dumps(output, indent=2)
1473 |         if args.output:
1474 |             with open(args.output, "w") as f:
1475 |                 f.write(text)
1476 |             print(f"Results written to {args.output}")
1477 |         else:
1478 |             print(text)
1479 |     else:
1480 |         # Print per-category reports
1481 |         for category, results in vocab_results.items():
1482 |             print_category_report(category, results, num_docs)
1483 | 
1484 |         if morpheme_results:
1485 |             print(f"\n{'═' * 78}")
1486 |             print("  MORPHEME STEM ANALYSIS")
1487 |             print(f"{'═' * 78}")
1488 |             for category, results in morpheme_results.items():
1489 |                 print_category_report(category, results, num_docs)
1490 | 
1491 |         # Print summary
1492 |         print_summary(vocab_results, morpheme_results, num_docs)
1493 | 
1494 |         # Save JSON alongside for programmatic use
1495 |         if args.output:
1496 |             output = {
1497 |                 "num_docs": num_docs,
1498 |                 "elapsed_s": elapsed,
1499 |                 "vocab": vocab_results,
1500 |                 "morphemes": morpheme_results,
1501 |             }
1502 |             with open(args.output, "w") as f:
1503 |                 json.dump(output, f, indent=2)
1504 |             print(f"\nJSON results saved to {args.output}")
1505 | 
1506 | 
1507 | if __name__ == "__main__":
1508 |     main()
```

## File: /Volumes/external/sources/nanochat/nanochat/tokenizer.py

- Extension: .py
- Language: python
- Size: 26210 bytes
- Created: 2026-04-06 22:29:59
- Modified: 2026-04-06 22:29:59

### Code

```python
  1 | """
  2 | BPE Tokenizer in the style of GPT-4.
  3 | 
  4 | Two implementations are available:
  5 | 1) HuggingFace Tokenizer that can do both training and inference but is really confusing
  6 | 2) Our own RustBPE Tokenizer for training and tiktoken for efficient inference
  7 | """
  8 | 
  9 | import os
 10 | import copy
 11 | import pickle
 12 | from functools import lru_cache
 13 | from typing import Any, cast
 14 | 
 15 | from tokenizers import Tokenizer as HFTokenizer
 16 | from tokenizers import Regex, decoders, pre_tokenizers
 17 | from tokenizers.models import BPE
 18 | from tokenizers.trainers import BpeTrainer
 19 | 
 20 | SPECIAL_TOKENS = [
 21 |     # every document begins with the Beginning of Sequence (BOS) token that delimits documents
 22 |     "<|bos|>",
 23 |     # tokens below are only used during finetuning to render Conversations into token ids
 24 |     "<|user_start|>",  # user messages
 25 |     "<|user_end|>",
 26 |     "<|assistant_start|>",  # assistant messages
 27 |     "<|assistant_end|>",
 28 |     "<|python_start|>",  # assistant invokes python REPL tool
 29 |     "<|python_end|>",
 30 |     "<|output_start|>",  # python REPL outputs back to assistant
 31 |     "<|output_end|>",
 32 | ]
 33 | 
 34 | # NOTE: this split pattern deviates from GPT-4 in that we use \p{N}{1,2} instead of \p{N}{1,3}
 35 | # I did this because I didn't want to "waste" too many tokens on numbers for smaller vocab sizes.
 36 | # I verified that 2 is the sweet spot for vocab size of 32K. 1 is a bit worse, 3 was worse still.
 37 | SPLIT_PATTERN = r"""'(?i:[sdmt]|ll|ve|re)|[^\r\n\p{L}\p{N}]?+\p{L}+|\p{N}{1,2}| ?[^\s\p{L}\p{N}]++[\r\n]*|\s*[\r\n]|\s+(?!\S)|\s+"""
 38 | 
 39 | # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 40 | # Generic GPT-4-style tokenizer based on HuggingFace Tokenizer
 41 | 
 42 | 
 43 | class HuggingFaceTokenizer:
 44 |     """Light wrapper around HuggingFace Tokenizer for some utilities"""
 45 | 
 46 |     def __init__(self, tokenizer):
 47 |         self.tokenizer = tokenizer
 48 | 
 49 |     @classmethod
 50 |     def from_pretrained(cls, hf_path):
 51 |         # init from a HuggingFace pretrained tokenizer (e.g. "gpt2")
 52 |         tokenizer = HFTokenizer.from_pretrained(hf_path)
 53 |         return cls(tokenizer)
 54 | 
 55 |     @classmethod
 56 |     def from_directory(cls, tokenizer_dir):
 57 |         # init from a local directory on disk (e.g. "out/tokenizer")
 58 |         tokenizer_path = os.path.join(tokenizer_dir, "tokenizer.json")
 59 |         tokenizer = HFTokenizer.from_file(tokenizer_path)
 60 |         return cls(tokenizer)
 61 | 
 62 |     @classmethod
 63 |     def train_from_iterator(cls, text_iterator, vocab_size):
 64 |         # train from an iterator of text
 65 |         # Configure the HuggingFace Tokenizer
 66 |         tokenizer = HFTokenizer(
 67 |             BPE(
 68 |                 byte_fallback=True,  # needed!
 69 |                 unk_token=None,
 70 |                 fuse_unk=False,
 71 |             )
 72 |         )
 73 |         backend_tokenizer = cast(Any, tokenizer)
 74 |         # Normalizer: None
 75 |         backend_tokenizer.normalizer = None
 76 |         # Pre-tokenizer: GPT-4 style
 77 |         # the regex pattern used by GPT-4 to split text into groups before BPE
 78 |         # NOTE: The pattern was changed from \p{N}{1,3} to \p{N}{1,2} because I suspect it is harmful to
 79 |         # very small models and smaller vocab sizes, because it is a little bit wasteful in the token space.
 80 |         # (but I haven't validated this! TODO)
 81 |         gpt4_split_regex = Regex(
 82 |             SPLIT_PATTERN
 83 |         )  # huggingface demands that you wrap it in Regex!!
 84 |         backend_tokenizer.pre_tokenizer = pre_tokenizers.Sequence(
 85 |             [
 86 |                 pre_tokenizers.Split(
 87 |                     pattern=gpt4_split_regex, behavior="isolated", invert=False
 88 |                 ),
 89 |                 pre_tokenizers.ByteLevel(add_prefix_space=False, use_regex=False),
 90 |             ]
 91 |         )
 92 |         # Decoder: ByteLevel (it pairs together with the ByteLevel pre-tokenizer)
 93 |         backend_tokenizer.decoder = decoders.ByteLevel()
 94 |         # Post-processor: None
 95 |         backend_tokenizer.post_processor = None
 96 |         # Trainer: BPE
 97 |         trainer_kwargs = {
 98 |             "vocab_size": vocab_size,
 99 |             "show_progress": True,
100 |             "min_frequency": 0,
101 |             "initial_alphabet": pre_tokenizers.ByteLevel.alphabet(),
102 |             "special_tokens": SPECIAL_TOKENS,
103 |         }
104 |         trainer = BpeTrainer(**cast(Any, trainer_kwargs))
105 |         # Kick off the training
106 |         tokenizer.train_from_iterator(text_iterator, trainer)
107 |         return cls(tokenizer)
108 | 
109 |     def get_vocab_size(self):
110 |         return self.tokenizer.get_vocab_size()
111 | 
112 |     def get_special_tokens(self):
113 |         special_tokens_map = self.tokenizer.get_added_tokens_decoder()
114 |         special_tokens = [w.content for w in special_tokens_map.values()]
115 |         return special_tokens
116 | 
117 |     def id_to_token(self, id):
118 |         return self.tokenizer.id_to_token(id)
119 | 
120 |     def _encode_one(self, text, prepend=None, append=None, num_threads=None):
121 |         # encode a single string
122 |         # prepend/append can be either a string of a special token or a token id directly.
123 |         # num_threads is ignored (only used by the nanochat Tokenizer for parallel encoding)
124 |         assert isinstance(text, str)
125 |         ids = []
126 |         if prepend is not None:
127 |             prepend_id = (
128 |                 prepend if isinstance(prepend, int) else self.encode_special(prepend)
129 |             )
130 |             ids.append(prepend_id)
131 |         ids.extend(self.tokenizer.encode(text, add_special_tokens=False).ids)
132 |         if append is not None:
133 |             append_id = (
134 |                 append if isinstance(append, int) else self.encode_special(append)
135 |             )
136 |             ids.append(append_id)
137 |         return ids
138 | 
139 |     def encode_special(self, text):
140 |         # encode a single special token via exact match
141 |         return self.tokenizer.token_to_id(text)
142 | 
143 |     def get_bos_token_id(self):
144 |         # Different HuggingFace models use different BOS tokens and there is little consistency
145 |         # 1) attempt to find a <|bos|> token
146 |         bos = self.encode_special("<|bos|>")
147 |         # 2) if that fails, attempt to find a <|endoftext|> token (e.g. GPT-2 models)
148 |         if bos is None:
149 |             bos = self.encode_special("<|endoftext|>")
150 |         # 3) if these fail, it's better to crash than to silently return None
151 |         assert bos is not None, "Failed to find BOS token in tokenizer"
152 |         return bos
153 | 
154 |     def encode(self, text, *args, **kwargs):
155 |         if isinstance(text, str):
156 |             return self._encode_one(text, *args, **kwargs)
157 |         elif isinstance(text, list):
158 |             return [self._encode_one(t, *args, **kwargs) for t in text]
159 |         else:
160 |             raise ValueError(f"Invalid input type: {type(text)}")
161 | 
162 |     def __call__(self, *args, **kwargs):
163 |         return self.encode(*args, **kwargs)
164 | 
165 |     def decode(self, ids):
166 |         return self.tokenizer.decode(ids, skip_special_tokens=False)
167 | 
168 |     def save(self, tokenizer_dir):
169 |         # save the tokenizer to disk
170 |         os.makedirs(tokenizer_dir, exist_ok=True)
171 |         tokenizer_path = os.path.join(tokenizer_dir, "tokenizer.json")
172 |         self.tokenizer.save(tokenizer_path)
173 |         print(f"Saved tokenizer to {tokenizer_path}")
174 | 
175 | 
176 | # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
177 | # Tokenizer based on rustbpe + tiktoken combo
178 | # NOTE: rustbpe and tiktoken are imported lazily inside methods to avoid
179 | # tainting the entire module when these optional deps aren't installed.
180 | 
181 | 
182 | class RustBPETokenizer:
183 |     """Light wrapper around tiktoken (for efficient inference) but train with rustbpe"""
184 | 
185 |     def __init__(self, enc, bos_token):
186 |         self.enc = enc
187 |         self.bos_token_id = self.encode_special(bos_token)
188 | 
189 |     @classmethod
190 |     def train_from_iterator(cls, text_iterator, vocab_size):
191 |         import rustbpe
192 | 
193 |         # 1) train using rustbpe
194 |         tokenizer = cast(Any, rustbpe).Tokenizer()
195 |         # the special tokens are inserted later in __init__, we don't train them here
196 |         vocab_size_no_special = vocab_size - len(SPECIAL_TOKENS)
197 |         assert vocab_size_no_special >= 256, (
198 |             f"vocab_size_no_special must be at least 256, got {vocab_size_no_special}"
199 |         )
200 |         tokenizer.train_from_iterator(
201 |             text_iterator, vocab_size_no_special, pattern=SPLIT_PATTERN
202 |         )
203 |         # 2) construct the associated tiktoken encoding for inference
204 |         import tiktoken
205 | 
206 |         pattern = tokenizer.get_pattern()
207 |         mergeable_ranks_list = tokenizer.get_mergeable_ranks()
208 |         mergeable_ranks = {bytes(k): v for k, v in mergeable_ranks_list}
209 |         tokens_offset = len(mergeable_ranks)
210 |         special_tokens = {
211 |             name: tokens_offset + i for i, name in enumerate(SPECIAL_TOKENS)
212 |         }
213 |         enc = tiktoken.Encoding(
214 |             name="rustbpe",
215 |             pat_str=pattern,
216 |             mergeable_ranks=mergeable_ranks,  # dict[bytes, int] (token bytes -> merge priority rank)
217 |             special_tokens=special_tokens,  # dict[str, int] (special token name -> token id)
218 |         )
219 |         return cls(enc, "<|bos|>")
220 | 
221 |     @classmethod
222 |     def from_directory(cls, tokenizer_dir):
223 |         pickle_path = os.path.join(tokenizer_dir, "tokenizer.pkl")
224 |         with open(pickle_path, "rb") as f:
225 |             enc = pickle.load(f)
226 |         return cls(enc, "<|bos|>")
227 | 
228 |     @classmethod
229 |     def from_pretrained(cls, tiktoken_name):
230 |         import tiktoken
231 | 
232 |         # https://github.com/openai/tiktoken/blob/eedc8563/tiktoken_ext/openai_public.py
233 |         enc = tiktoken.get_encoding(tiktoken_name)
234 |         # tiktoken calls the special document delimiter token "<|endoftext|>"
235 |         # yes this is confusing because this token is almost always PREPENDED to the beginning of the document
236 |         # it most often is used to signal the start of a new sequence to the LLM during inference etc.
237 |         # so in nanoChat we always use "<|bos|>" short for "beginning of sequence", but historically it is often called "<|endoftext|>".
238 |         return cls(enc, "<|endoftext|>")
239 | 
240 |     def get_vocab_size(self):
241 |         return self.enc.n_vocab
242 | 
243 |     def get_special_tokens(self):
244 |         return self.enc.special_tokens_set
245 | 
246 |     def id_to_token(self, id):
247 |         return self.enc.decode([id])
248 | 
249 |     @lru_cache(maxsize=32)
250 |     def encode_special(self, text):
251 |         return self.enc.encode_single_token(text)
252 | 
253 |     def get_bos_token_id(self):
254 |         return self.bos_token_id
255 | 
256 |     def encode(self, text, prepend=None, append=None, num_threads=8):
257 |         # text can be either a string or a list of strings
258 |         prepend_id: int | None = None
259 |         append_id: int | None = None
260 | 
261 |         if prepend is not None:
262 |             prepend_id = (
263 |                 prepend if isinstance(prepend, int) else self.encode_special(prepend)
264 |             )
265 |         if append is not None:
266 |             append_id = (
267 |                 append if isinstance(append, int) else self.encode_special(append)
268 |             )
269 | 
270 |         if isinstance(text, str):
271 |             ids = self.enc.encode_ordinary(text)
272 |             if prepend is not None:
273 |                 ids.insert(0, prepend_id)  # TODO: slightly inefficient here? :( hmm
274 |             if append is not None:
275 |                 ids.append(append_id)
276 |         elif isinstance(text, list):
277 |             ids = self.enc.encode_ordinary_batch(text, num_threads=num_threads)
278 |             if prepend is not None:
279 |                 for ids_row in ids:
280 |                     ids_row.insert(0, prepend_id)  # TODO: same
281 |             if append is not None:
282 |                 for ids_row in ids:
283 |                     ids_row.append(append_id)
284 |         else:
285 |             raise ValueError(f"Invalid input type: {type(text)}")
286 | 
287 |         return ids
288 | 
289 |     def __call__(self, *args, **kwargs):
290 |         return self.encode(*args, **kwargs)
291 | 
292 |     def decode(self, ids):
293 |         return self.enc.decode(ids)
294 | 
295 |     def save(self, tokenizer_dir):
296 |         # save the encoding object to disk
297 |         os.makedirs(tokenizer_dir, exist_ok=True)
298 |         pickle_path = os.path.join(tokenizer_dir, "tokenizer.pkl")
299 |         with open(pickle_path, "wb") as f:
300 |             pickle.dump(self.enc, f)
301 |         print(f"Saved tokenizer encoding to {pickle_path}")
302 | 
303 |     def render_conversation(self, conversation, max_tokens=2048):
304 |         """
305 |         Tokenize a single Chat conversation (which we call a "doc" or "document" here).
306 |         Returns:
307 |         - ids: list[int] is a list of token ids of this rendered conversation
308 |         - mask: list[int] of same length, mask = 1 for tokens that the Assistant is expected to train on.
309 |         """
310 |         # ids, masks that we will return and a helper function to help build them up.
311 |         ids, mask = [], []
312 | 
313 |         def add_tokens(token_ids, mask_val):
314 |             if isinstance(token_ids, int):
315 |                 token_ids = [token_ids]
316 |             ids.extend(token_ids)
317 |             mask.extend([mask_val] * len(token_ids))
318 | 
319 |         # sometimes the first message is a system message...
320 |         # => just merge it with the second (user) message
321 |         if conversation["messages"][0]["role"] == "system":
322 |             # some conversation surgery is necessary here for now...
323 |             conversation = copy.deepcopy(conversation)  # avoid mutating the original
324 |             messages = conversation["messages"]
325 |             assert messages[1]["role"] == "user", (
326 |                 "System message must be followed by a user message"
327 |             )
328 |             messages[1]["content"] = (
329 |                 messages[0]["content"] + "\n\n" + messages[1]["content"]
330 |             )
331 |             messages = messages[1:]
332 |         else:
333 |             messages = conversation["messages"]
334 |         assert len(messages) >= 1, f"Conversation has less than 1 message: {messages}"
335 | 
336 |         # fetch all the special tokens we need
337 |         bos = self.get_bos_token_id()
338 |         user_start, user_end = (
339 |             self.encode_special("<|user_start|>"),
340 |             self.encode_special("<|user_end|>"),
341 |         )
342 |         assistant_start, assistant_end = (
343 |             self.encode_special("<|assistant_start|>"),
344 |             self.encode_special("<|assistant_end|>"),
345 |         )
346 |         python_start, python_end = (
347 |             self.encode_special("<|python_start|>"),
348 |             self.encode_special("<|python_end|>"),
349 |         )
350 |         output_start, output_end = (
351 |             self.encode_special("<|output_start|>"),
352 |             self.encode_special("<|output_end|>"),
353 |         )
354 | 
355 |         # now we can tokenize the conversation
356 |         add_tokens(bos, 0)
357 |         for i, message in enumerate(messages):
358 |             # some sanity checking here around assumptions, to prevent footguns
359 |             must_be_from = "user" if i % 2 == 0 else "assistant"
360 |             assert message["role"] == must_be_from, (
361 |                 f"Message {i} is from {message['role']} but should be from {must_be_from}"
362 |             )
363 | 
364 |             # content can be either a simple string or a list of parts (e.g. containing tool calls)
365 |             content = message["content"]
366 | 
367 |             if message["role"] == "user":
368 |                 assert isinstance(content, str), (
369 |                     "User messages are simply expected to be strings"
370 |                 )
371 |                 value_ids = self.encode(content)
372 |                 add_tokens(user_start, 0)
373 |                 add_tokens(value_ids, 0)
374 |                 add_tokens(user_end, 0)
375 |             elif message["role"] == "assistant":
376 |                 add_tokens(assistant_start, 0)
377 |                 if isinstance(content, str):
378 |                     # simple string => simply add the tokens
379 |                     value_ids = self.encode(content)
380 |                     add_tokens(value_ids, 1)
381 |                 elif isinstance(content, list):
382 |                     for part in content:
383 |                         value_ids = self.encode(part["text"])
384 |                         if part["type"] == "text":
385 |                             # string part => simply add the tokens
386 |                             add_tokens(value_ids, 1)
387 |                         elif part["type"] == "python":
388 |                             # python tool call => add the tokens inside <|python_start|> and <|python_end|>
389 |                             add_tokens(python_start, 1)
390 |                             add_tokens(value_ids, 1)
391 |                             add_tokens(python_end, 1)
392 |                         elif part["type"] == "python_output":
393 |                             # python output => add the tokens inside <|output_start|> and <|output_end|>
394 |                             # none of these tokens are supervised because the tokens come from Python at test time
395 |                             add_tokens(output_start, 0)
396 |                             add_tokens(value_ids, 0)
397 |                             add_tokens(output_end, 0)
398 |                         else:
399 |                             raise ValueError(f"Unknown part type: {part['type']}")
400 |                 else:
401 |                     raise ValueError(f"Unknown content type: {type(content)}")
402 |                 add_tokens(assistant_end, 1)
403 | 
404 |         # truncate to max_tokens tokens MAX (helps prevent OOMs)
405 |         ids = ids[:max_tokens]
406 |         mask = mask[:max_tokens]
407 |         return ids, mask
408 | 
409 |     def visualize_tokenization(self, ids, mask, with_token_id=False):
410 |         """Small helper function useful in debugging: visualize the tokenization of render_conversation"""
411 |         RED = "\033[91m"
412 |         GREEN = "\033[92m"
413 |         RESET = "\033[0m"
414 |         GRAY = "\033[90m"
415 |         tokens = []
416 |         for i, (token_id, mask_val) in enumerate(zip(ids, mask)):
417 |             token_str = self.decode([token_id])
418 |             color = GREEN if mask_val == 1 else RED
419 |             tokens.append(f"{color}{token_str}{RESET}")
420 |             if with_token_id:
421 |                 tokens.append(f"{GRAY}({token_id}){RESET}")
422 |         return "|".join(tokens)
423 | 
424 |     def render_for_completion(self, conversation):
425 |         """
426 |         Used during Reinforcement Learning. In that setting, we want to
427 |         render the conversation priming the Assistant for a completion.
428 |         Unlike the Chat SFT case, we don't need to return the mask.
429 |         """
430 |         # We have some surgery to do: we need to pop the last message (of the Assistant)
431 |         conversation = copy.deepcopy(conversation)  # avoid mutating the original
432 |         messages = conversation["messages"]
433 |         assert messages[-1]["role"] == "assistant", (
434 |             "Last message must be from the Assistant"
435 |         )
436 |         messages.pop()  # remove the last message (of the Assistant) inplace
437 | 
438 |         # Now tokenize the conversation
439 |         ids, mask = self.render_conversation(conversation)
440 | 
441 |         # Finally, to prime the Assistant for a completion, append the Assistant start token
442 |         assistant_start = self.encode_special("<|assistant_start|>")
443 |         ids.append(assistant_start)
444 |         return ids
445 | 
446 | 
447 | # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
448 | # nanochat-specific convenience functions
449 | 
450 | # C++ keywords that should be single tokens with the C++ tokenizer
451 | _CPP_KEYWORDS = [
452 |     "class",
453 |     "struct",
454 |     "namespace",
455 |     "template",
456 |     "virtual",
457 |     "override",
458 |     "public",
459 |     "private",
460 |     "protected",
461 |     "const",
462 |     "static",
463 |     "inline",
464 |     "void",
465 |     "int",
466 |     "bool",
467 |     "char",
468 |     "float",
469 |     "double",
470 |     "auto",
471 |     "return",
472 | ]
473 | 
474 | 
475 | def verify_cpp_tokenizer(tokenizer, sample_code=None):
476 |     """
477 |     Verify that the C++ tokenizer is correctly tokenizing C++ keywords as single tokens.
478 | 
479 |     Call this at training start to confirm the tokenizer is appropriate for your data.
480 |     Returns True if C++ keywords are single tokens, False otherwise.
481 |     Prints a warning if the C++ tokenizer appears to be splitting keywords.
482 |     """
483 |     if sample_code is None:
484 |         # Use a representative C++ snippet
485 |         sample_code = (
486 |             "class MyClass { public: virtual void process() const override; };"
487 |         )
488 | 
489 |     tokenizer.encode(sample_code)
490 | 
491 |     # Check if common C++ keywords are single tokens
492 |     keywords_found = 0
493 |     keywords_single = 0
494 | 
495 |     for keyword in _CPP_KEYWORDS[:10]:  # Check first 10 keywords
496 |         keyword_tokens = tokenizer.encode(keyword)
497 |         if len(keyword_tokens) == 1:
498 |             keywords_single += 1
499 |         keywords_found += 1
500 | 
501 |     is_cpp_optimized = keywords_single >= 8  # At least 80% should be single tokens
502 | 
503 |     if not is_cpp_optimized:
504 |         print(
505 |             f"WARNING: C++ tokenizer check: only {keywords_single}/{keywords_found} C++ keywords "
506 |             f"are single tokens. This tokenizer may not be optimal for C++ code."
507 |         )
508 |         print("NOTE: NANOCHAT_CPP_TOKENIZER=1 is now the default.")
509 |         print(
510 |             "      Set NANOCHAT_CPP_TOKENIZER=0 to use RustBPE tokenizer for non-C++ training."
511 |         )
512 | 
513 |     return is_cpp_optimized
514 | 
515 | 
516 | def get_tokenizer_dir():
517 |     """Resolve tokenizer assets independently from the checkpoint base dir."""
518 |     explicit = os.environ.get("NANOCHAT_TOKENIZER_DIR")
519 |     if explicit:
520 |         return explicit
521 | 
522 |     from nanochat.common import get_base_dir
523 |     from nanochat.path_utils import get_writable_cache_root
524 | 
525 |     base_dir = get_base_dir()
526 |     primary = os.path.join(base_dir, "tokenizer")
527 |     if os.path.exists(os.path.join(primary, "tokenizer.json")) or os.path.exists(
528 |         os.path.join(primary, "tokenizer.pkl")
529 |     ):
530 |         return primary
531 | 
532 |     cache_root = get_writable_cache_root()
533 |     cache_candidate = os.path.join(cache_root, "tokenizer")
534 |     if os.path.exists(os.path.join(cache_candidate, "tokenizer.json")) or os.path.exists(
535 |         os.path.join(cache_candidate, "tokenizer.pkl")
536 |     ):
537 |         return cache_candidate
538 | 
539 |     repo_root = os.path.abspath(os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), ".."))
540 |     repo_candidate = os.path.join(repo_root, ".cache", "tokenizer")
541 |     if os.path.exists(os.path.join(repo_candidate, "tokenizer.json")) or os.path.exists(
542 |         os.path.join(repo_candidate, "tokenizer.pkl")
543 |     ):
544 |         return repo_candidate
545 | 
546 |     return primary
547 | 
548 | 
549 | def get_tokenizer():
550 |     """
551 |     Get the default nanochat tokenizer.
552 | 
553 |     C++ tokenizer (tokenizer.json) is now the DEFAULT for C++ code training.
554 |     Set NANOCHAT_CPP_TOKENIZER=0 to disable and use RustBPE tokenizer instead.
555 |     """
556 |     tokenizer_dir = get_tokenizer_dir()
557 | 
558 |     # C++ tokenizer is now the DEFAULT
559 |     # Set NANOCHAT_CPP_TOKENIZER=0 to disable
560 |     use_cpp = os.environ.get("NANOCHAT_CPP_TOKENIZER", "1") != "0"
561 | 
562 |     cpp_tokenizer_path = os.path.join(tokenizer_dir, "tokenizer.json")
563 |     if use_cpp and os.path.exists(cpp_tokenizer_path):
564 |         from nanochat.cpp_tokenizer import CppTokenizer
565 | 
566 |         return CppTokenizer(tokenizer_dir)
567 | 
568 |     # Fallback to RustBPE tokenizer
569 |     import warnings
570 | 
571 |     pkl_path = os.path.join(tokenizer_dir, "tokenizer.pkl")
572 |     if os.path.exists(pkl_path):
573 |         warnings.warn(
574 |             f"tokenizer.json not found at {cpp_tokenizer_path}, "
575 |             f"falling back to RustBPE tokenizer (tokenizer.pkl). "
576 |             f"This is a DIFFERENT tokenizer with potentially different vocab. "
577 |             f"Set NANOCHAT_CPP_TOKENIZER=0 to suppress this warning if intentional.",
578 |             UserWarning,
579 |             stacklevel=2,
580 |         )
581 |     if os.path.exists(pkl_path):
582 |         return RustBPETokenizer.from_directory(tokenizer_dir)
583 | 
584 |     raise FileNotFoundError(
585 |         "No tokenizer assets found. Expected tokenizer.json or tokenizer.pkl under "
586 |         f"{tokenizer_dir}. Set NANOCHAT_TOKENIZER_DIR to a valid tokenizer asset directory."
587 |     )
588 | 
589 | 
590 | def _compute_token_bytes_from_tokenizer(tokenizer):
591 |     """Compute token_bytes tensor directly from tokenizer (no file needed).
592 | 
593 |     Returns 1D int32 tensor of shape (vocab_size,) where each entry is the
594 |     UTF-8 byte length of that token, or 0 for special/unknown tokens.
595 | 
596 |     Uses the underlying HuggingFace tokenizer's decode when available, because
597 |     byte-level BPE tokenizers store vocab keys in byte-level encoding (e.g.
598 |     'Ġhello' for ' hello') whose UTF-8 byte count differs from the actual
599 |     token's byte count. The HF decode path correctly reverses byte-level encoding.
600 |     """
601 |     import torch
602 | 
603 |     vocab_size = tokenizer.get_vocab_size()
604 |     token_bytes = torch.zeros(vocab_size, dtype=torch.int32)
605 | 
606 |     # For CppTokenizer / HuggingFaceTokenizer, use underlying HF tokenizer
607 |     # which correctly handles byte-level BPE decoding
608 |     hf_tok = getattr(tokenizer, "_tokenizer", None) or getattr(
609 |         tokenizer, "tokenizer", None
610 |     )
611 | 
612 |     for tid in range(vocab_size):
613 |         try:
614 |             if hf_tok is not None:
615 |                 decoded = hf_tok.decode([tid], skip_special_tokens=False)
616 |             else:
617 |                 decoded = tokenizer.decode([tid])
618 |         except Exception:
619 |             continue
620 |         if not decoded:
621 |             continue
622 |         # Special tokens decode to their tag strings (e.g. "<|bos|>") — skip them
623 |         if decoded.startswith("<") and decoded.endswith(">") and len(decoded) > 2:
624 |             continue
625 |         token_bytes[tid] = len(decoded.encode("utf-8"))
626 |     return token_bytes
627 | 
628 | 
629 | def get_token_bytes(device="cpu", tokenizer=None):
630 |     """Get token_bytes tensor for BPB evaluation.
631 | 
632 |     If tokenizer is provided, computes token_bytes directly from it (always
633 |     matches vocab_size exactly). Otherwise falls back to loading token_bytes.pt.
634 |     """
635 |     import torch
636 | 
637 |     if tokenizer is not None:
638 |         token_bytes = _compute_token_bytes_from_tokenizer(tokenizer)
639 |         if str(device) != "cpu":
640 |             token_bytes = token_bytes.to(device)
641 |         return token_bytes
642 | 
643 |     # Fallback: load from file
644 |     tokenizer_dir = get_tokenizer_dir()
645 |     token_bytes_path = os.path.join(tokenizer_dir, "token_bytes.pt")
646 |     assert os.path.exists(token_bytes_path), (
647 |         f"Token bytes not found at {token_bytes_path}? It gets written by tok_train.py"
648 |     )
649 |     with open(token_bytes_path, "rb") as f:
650 |         # Load to CPU first, then move to target device
651 |         # (XLA devices don't support direct torch.load map_location)
652 |         token_bytes = torch.load(f, map_location="cpu")
653 |     # Handle 2D token_bytes (raw byte values) vs 1D (byte counts)
654 |     # Some tokenizer builds save raw bytes as (vocab_size, max_bytes) uint8
655 |     # while loss_eval expects 1D byte counts (vocab_size,)
656 |     if token_bytes.dim() == 2:
657 |         # Convert 2D raw bytes to 1D byte counts: count non-zero entries per row
658 |         token_bytes = (token_bytes > 0).sum(dim=1).to(torch.int32)
659 |     if str(device) != "cpu":
660 |         token_bytes = token_bytes.to(device)
661 |     return token_bytes
```

  1 | /// Core analysis engine: fast identifier extraction + token counting.
  2 | ///
  3 | /// Key design: O(n) single-pass scanner for identifiers, then O(1) HashSet lookups.
  4 | /// No regex in hot path. Hand-written state machine for identifier extraction.
  5 | 
  6 | use std::collections::HashMap;
  7 | 
  8 | /// Per-document analysis result (stack-allocated counts, no heap until merge).
  9 | pub struct DocResult {
 10 |     /// Identifier -> count in this document
 11 |     pub ident_counts: HashMap<String, u64>,
 12 |     /// $-prefixed operator -> count
 13 |     pub dollar_counts: HashMap<String, u64>,
 14 |     /// SQL keyword (in string literals) -> count
 15 |     pub sql_keyword_counts: HashMap<String, u64>,
 16 |     /// Morpheme component -> count
 17 |     pub morpheme_counts: HashMap<String, u64>,
 18 |     /// Identifier naming style counts
 19 |     pub style_counts: StyleCounts,
 20 |     /// Namespace-qualified identifier counts (std::, boost::, etc.)
 21 |     pub namespace_counts: HashMap<String, u64>,
 22 |     /// Total identifier count
 23 |     pub total_idents: u64,
 24 |     /// Total unique identifiers
 25 |     pub unique_idents: u64,
 26 |     /// Sum of identifier lengths
 27 |     pub total_ident_len: u64,
 28 |     /// Whitespace/comment/unicode analysis
 29 |     pub content_stats: ContentStats,
 30 | }
 31 | 
 32 | /// Whitespace, comment, and unicode content statistics for BPE design.
 33 | #[derive(Default, Clone)]
 34 | pub struct ContentStats {
 35 |     /// Total bytes in document
 36 |     pub total_bytes: u64,
 37 |     /// Bytes in comments (// and /* */)
 38 |     pub comment_bytes: u64,
 39 |     /// Bytes in string literals
 40 |     pub string_bytes: u64,
 41 |     /// Number of line comments (//)
 42 |     pub line_comments: u64,
 43 |     /// Number of block comments (/* */)
 44 |     pub block_comments: u64,
 45 |     /// Lines that are only whitespace
 46 |     pub blank_lines: u64,
 47 |     /// Total lines
 48 |     pub total_lines: u64,
 49 |     /// Whitespace statistics
 50 |     pub single_spaces: u64,
 51 |     pub multi_spaces: u64,    // runs of 2+ spaces
 52 |     pub single_tabs: u64,
 53 |     pub multi_tabs: u64,      // runs of 2+ tabs
 54 |     pub mixed_indent: u64,    // lines with both spaces and tabs before first non-ws
 55 |     /// Total indentation levels detected (spaces-based)
 56 |     pub indent_2: u64,        // 2-space indent
 57 |     pub indent_4: u64,        // 4-space indent
 58 |     pub indent_8: u64,        // 8-space indent (or 2-tab)
 59 |     pub tab_indent: u64,      // tab-based indent
 60 |     /// Unicode content
 61 |     pub non_ascii_bytes: u64,
 62 |     pub utf8_chars: u64,      // multi-byte UTF-8 sequences
 63 |     /// Comments containing non-ASCII (likely non-English)
 64 |     pub non_ascii_comments: u64,
 65 |     /// Docs with any non-ASCII comment content
 66 |     pub has_non_ascii_comment: bool,
 67 |     /// Detected non-English comment samples (first few)
 68 |     pub non_english_samples: Vec<String>,
 69 | }

 71 | impl ContentStats {
 72 |     pub fn merge(&mut self, other: &ContentStats) {
 73 |         self.total_bytes += other.total_bytes;
 74 |         self.comment_bytes += other.comment_bytes;
 75 |         self.string_bytes += other.string_bytes;
 76 |         self.line_comments += other.line_comments;
 77 |         self.block_comments += other.block_comments;
 78 |         self.blank_lines += other.blank_lines;
 79 |         self.total_lines += other.total_lines;
 80 |         self.single_spaces += other.single_spaces;
 81 |         self.multi_spaces += other.multi_spaces;
 82 |         self.single_tabs += other.single_tabs;
 83 |         self.multi_tabs += other.multi_tabs;
 84 |         self.mixed_indent += other.mixed_indent;
 85 |         self.indent_2 += other.indent_2;
 86 |         self.indent_4 += other.indent_4;
 87 |         self.indent_8 += other.indent_8;
 88 |         self.tab_indent += other.tab_indent;
 89 |         self.non_ascii_bytes += other.non_ascii_bytes;
 90 |         self.utf8_chars += other.utf8_chars;
 91 |         self.non_ascii_comments += other.non_ascii_comments;
